The Joint Effect of Quantization and Sampling Temperature on LLM Safety Alignment: A Factorial Analysis
Authors: Hari Prasad, Ritam Pal
First: 2026-06-28T19:52:25+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-15T10:24:21+00:00
Comments: 11 pages, 5 Figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Modern LLM deployments often combine quantization with higher sampling temperatures to reduce cost, latency, or repetition, yet safety evaluations usually treat these as fixed implementation details. We test whether models that are safe at FP16 with greedy decoding remain safe after quantization and stochastic sampling, or whether the two factors amplify each other. We evaluate 8 instruction-tuned models from five families across 3 precisions and 6 temperatures, covering 144 configurations on 7 harmfulness benchmarks and generating about 2.0 million responses, which are scored by a six-judge safety ensemble. Contrary to concerns that low-bit deployment erodes alignment, we find that standard quantization is approximately safety-neutral: for 7 of 8 models, AWQ INT4 keeps attack success within about 1.6 percentage points of FP16 or lowers it, with clear degradation only for SmolLM3-3B (34.5% to 44.1%). However, the larger risk comes from sampling: higher temperatures sharply increase decision instability, with DFR reaching 41.9% at T = 1.0, even when average ASR changes only modestly. The two factors do not compound: our Compound Degradation Index remains sub-additive (-0.071 to +0.018), indicating that quantization partially offsets rather than amplifies temperature-induced degradation. Finally, a per-benchmark breakdown shows that single-benchmark evaluation badly understates risk: several models scoring 0% on AdvBench exceed 80% on ManyHarm. Standard INT4/INT8 quantization can therefore be reasonable for well-aligned models, but safety claims should report multi-sample stability across multiple benchmarks rather than rely on a single benchmark at greedy decoding.
中文标题/摘要
标题：量化与采样温度对大型语言模型安全对齐的联合影响：一个因子分析
现代大型语言模型的部署通常将量化与更高的采样温度结合，以降低成本、延迟或重复性，但安全评估通常将这些视为固定的实施细节。我们测试在FP16下使用贪婪解码安全的模型在量化和随机采样后是否仍然安全，或者这两个因素是否相互放大。我们评估了来自五个家族的8个指令调优模型，涵盖3种精度和6种温度，涉及144种配置，在7个有害性基准上生成约200万条响应，并由六名评审组成的安全团队进行评分。与低位部署会削弱对齐的担忧相反，我们发现标准量化大致是安全中性的：对于8个模型中的7个，AWQ INT4将攻击成功率保持在FP16的约1.6个百分点以内或降低，仅对SmolLM3-3B（34.5%到44.1%）出现明显降级。然而，较大的风险来自采样：更高的温度显著增加决策不稳定性，在T = 1.0时DFR达到41.9%，即使平均ASR变化仅适度。这两个因素并不相互叠加：我们的复合降级指数保持在亚加性范围内（-0.071到+0.018），表明量化部分抵消而非放大温度引起的降级。最后，逐基准的细分显示，单基准评估严重低估了风险：几个在AdvBench上得分为0%的模型在ManyHarm上超过80%。因此，标准INT4/INT8量化对于对齐良好的模型是合理的，但安全声明应报告多个基准的多样本稳定性，而不是依赖于贪婪解码的单一基准。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the safety alignment of large language models (LLMs) in the context of modern deployments that utilize quantization and higher sampling temperatures, which are often treated as fixed parameters in safety evaluations. Previous methods typically overlook the interaction between quantization and sampling temperature, leading to potential misassessments of model safety. The proposed approach systematically evaluates the safety of eight instruction-tuned models across various precisions and temperatures, revealing that while standard quantization is generally safety-neutral, higher sampling temperatures significantly increase decision instability. The study's contribution lies in its factorial analysis of 144 configurations across seven harmfulness benchmarks, demonstrating that quantization does not exacerbate risks from sampling temperature and highlighting the importance of multi-benchmark evaluations for accurate safety assessments. The findings indicate that standard INT4/INT8 quantization can be acceptable for well-aligned models, but safety evaluations should consider stability across multiple samples and benchmarks rather than relying on single-benchmark assessments.
本研究探讨了大型语言模型（LLMs）在现代部署中安全对齐的问题，尤其是在量化和较高采样温度的背景下，这些因素通常在安全评估中被视为固定参数。以往的方法未能充分评估这些因素的综合影响，可能导致对模型安全性的误解。所提出的方法系统地评估了八个指令调优模型在不同精度和温度下的安全性，结果表明标准量化通常是安全中性的，而较高的采样温度显著增加了决策的不稳定性。该论文的贡献在于证明量化并不会加剧与温度变化相关的风险，并强调多基准评估在准确安全评估中的重要性。研究方法涉及在七个有害性基准上对144种配置进行因子分析，得出的见解支持了标准量化对于良好对齐模型的可接受性，前提是安全评估考虑多个样本和基准的稳定性。
Minionese: Comprehensive Benchmark and Mechanistic Study of Multilingual LLM Safety
Authors: Chigozirim Ifebi, Brent Kong, Ayushi Mehrotra
First: 2026-07-11T04:13:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-11T04:13:42+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models remains brittle across languages: prompts reliably refused in English can elicit harmful compliance in non-English and low-resource settings. We introduce \textsc{Minionese}, a multilingual jailbreak benchmark spanning 18 languages, 4 resource tiers, and 4 perturbation types (standard translation, code-switching, transliteration, and translationese), paired with a geometric mechanistic analysis of refusal failure across language tiers. We show that each attack type produces a distinct vulnerability profile: transliteration vulnerability is mediated by script identity, code-switching maintains effectiveness through the lowest-resource tier, and a sharp safety regime transition between Tiers 2 and 3 is consistent across all models. Mechanistically, low-resource jailbreaks succeed by routing harmful content through a geometrically misaligned subspace that projects insufficiently onto the refusal directions, leaving the refusal mechanism intact but untriggered. These findings show that English-only safety evaluations are insufficient; they require accounting for script family, perturbation type, and per-language alignment coverage. The benchmark and analysis code is at https://github.com/Brentkong/Minionese-Comprehensive-Benchmark-and-Mechanistic-Study-of-Multilingual-LLM-Safety.git.
中文标题/摘要
标题：小黄人语：多语言大型语言模型安全性的综合基准与机制研究
大型语言模型的安全对齐在不同语言间仍然脆弱：在英语中可靠拒绝的提示在非英语和低资源环境中可能引发有害的顺从。我们引入了\textsc{Minionese}，这是一个涵盖18种语言、4个资源层级和4种扰动类型（标准翻译、代码切换、音译和翻译语）的多语言越狱基准，并结合了对不同语言层级拒绝失败的几何机制分析。我们展示了每种攻击类型产生独特的脆弱性特征：音译脆弱性受脚本身份的影响，代码切换在最低资源层级中保持有效，而第2层和第3层之间的安全机制转变在所有模型中是一致的。从机制上讲，低资源越狱通过将有害内容引导到几何上不对齐的子空间中成功，该子空间在拒绝方向上投影不足，保持拒绝机制完好但未被触发。这些发现表明，仅依赖英语的安全评估是不够的；它们需要考虑脚本家族、扰动类型和每种语言的对齐覆盖。基准和分析代码可在https://github.com/Brentkong/Minionese-Comprehensive-Benchmark-and-Mechanistic-Study-of-Multilingual-LLM-Safety.git找到。

Mechanistic Interpretability of LLM Jailbreaks via Internal Attribution Graphs
Authors: Anupam Wagle, Ifrat Ikhtear Uddin, Chaowei Zhang, Longwei Wang
First: 2026-07-08T20:31:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-08T20:31:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) exhibit remarkable capabilities but remain highly vulnerable to adversarial prompts and jailbreak attacks. Existing approaches primarily analyze these failures through input-output behaviors or attribution methods, offering limited insight into how adversarial perturbations alter the model's internal reasoning. Consequently, the mechanisms underlying unsafe or incorrect behaviors remain poorly understood. We introduce a mechanistic framework for diagnosing LLM vulnerabilities using paired internal computation graphs, which represent prompt-specific inference as structured causal interactions among latent features. By constructing and aligning computation graphs for clean and attacked prompts, we reveal that adversarial attacks induce systematic transformations of internal reasoning, including suppression of safety-relevant components, emergence of attack-specific features, and rerouting of computation paths. Building on this representation, we propose a unified framework that (i) decomposes computation into invariant, suppressed, and emergent structures, (ii) identifies recurring vulnerability motifs associated with failure modes, and (iii) performs causal interventions on nodes, paths, and subgraphs to directly evaluate their contributions to attack success. This enables a transition from descriptive attribution to causal diagnosis of model failures. Experiments across multiple open-source LLMs and diverse adversarial and jailbreak benchmarks demonstrate that structural deviations in internal computation graphs strongly correlate with unsafe behaviors. Furthermore, targeted interventions on identified vulnerability motifs improve model robustness, establishing internal computation graphs as a principled foundation for understanding, diagnosing, and mitigating LLM vulnerabilities.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过内部归因图对LLM越狱的机制性可解释性
大型语言模型（LLMs）展现出显著的能力，但仍然对对抗性提示和越狱攻击高度脆弱。现有方法主要通过输入输出行为或归因方法分析这些失败，提供的见解有限，无法揭示对抗性扰动如何改变模型的内部推理。因此，导致不安全或不正确行为的机制仍然不清楚。我们引入了一种机制框架，通过配对的内部计算图来诊断LLM的脆弱性，这些图将特定提示的推理表示为潜在特征之间的结构因果交互。通过构建和对齐干净和攻击提示的计算图，我们揭示了对抗性攻击引发的内部推理的系统性转变，包括安全相关组件的抑制、攻击特征的出现和计算路径的重定向。在此表示的基础上，我们提出了一个统一框架，(i) 将计算分解为不变、抑制和新兴结构，(ii) 识别与失败模式相关的重复脆弱性特征，(iii) 对节点、路径和子图进行因果干预，以直接评估它们对攻击成功的贡献。这使得从描述性归因转向模型失败的因果诊断。针对多个开源LLM和多样的对抗性及越狱基准的实验表明，内部计算图的结构偏差与不安全行为强相关。此外，对识别出的脆弱性特征进行有针对性的干预提高了模型的鲁棒性，确立了内部计算图作为理解、诊断和减轻LLM脆弱性的原则基础。

Predicting LLM Safety Before Release by Simulating Deployment
Authors: Marcus Williams, Hannah Sheahan, Cameron Raymond, Tomek Korbak, Deng Pan, Peilin Yang, Leon Maksin, Ningyi Xie, Phillip Guo, Ian Kivlichan, Micah Carroll
First: 2026-07-08T09:17:47+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-08T09:17:47+00:00
Comments: 31 pages
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Pre-deployment safety evaluations aim to inform the downstream risks of releasing a new AI model. Yet most evaluations provide limited evidence about how often undesired model behavior will occur in deployment: they generally have insufficient coverage, are unrepresentative, and are generally recognizable as tests. To address these concerns, we study a simple way to simulate a model deployment: starting from de-identified conversations from a previous model deployment, we hold fixed the initial conversation prefix and regenerate the next response using a candidate model. The resulting responses can then both be audited for novel misalignments and used to estimate the prevalence of model misbehavior before deployment. We evaluate deployment simulation across four GPT-5-series deployments, using registered, outcome-blinded predictions for GPT-5.4 and retrospective analyses of three earlier releases. We find that deployment simulation produces informative estimates of post-deployment misbehavior rates and outperforms baselines based on adversarially selected production data; its evaluation-awareness point estimates were also much closer to production traffic than those from traditional evaluations. We also identify the realism of tool resampling as a central challenge for further improving predictions and share results suggesting that this challenge is surmountable even in complex tool-use settings. Finally, we show that deployment simulation can be seeded from public chat datasets and remain informative about production misbehavior rates, suggesting a path for external researchers to run deployment-grounded evaluations without access to private production logs. Overall, deployment simulation helps evaluators forecast how language models will behave in the real world and supports more quantitative assessment of deployment risk.

Operational Reframing and Approval-Framed Delegation in Multi-Agent LLM Safety
Authors: Lifei Liu, Haoran Yu, Xiaochong Jiang, Su Wang, Pin Qian, Yihang Chen
First: 2026-07-08T07:31:37+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-08T07:31:37+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluations of multi-agent LLM systems often compare a direct prompt with a planner-executor pipeline and report the difference as a single "pipeline effect." We argue that this aggregate is difficult to interpret because it conflates three mechanisms: harmful intent may be reframed as plausible operational work, the planner may refuse or transform the request, and the executor may act under delegation prompts implying prior approval. To separate these factors, we introduce a five-condition controlled contrast design, evaluated on 30 synthetic harmful scenarios and an exploratory external validation set from four agent-safety benchmarks using LLM-judged compliance. Our results show that aggregate pipeline safety is not a stable architectural property. Operational reframing is the most portable risk signal, increasing compliance for GPT, Gemini, and DeepSeek across both scenario sets, while Claude is comparatively resistant. Planner behavior can offset this risk mainly through refusal; however, when the planner produces executable steps, the executor may become more compliant than under the direct operational baseline. Approval-framed delegation is sensitive to prompt design, model pairing, and scenario source, and a skeptical executor prompt sharply reduces compliance. Raw-direct model rankings can also mispredict deployed planner-executor behavior. Gemini is safest under raw direct prompts in the primary set yet shows the largest amplification with a Claude planner, rising from 8.9 percent to 38.9 percent compliance. GPTs near-zero aggregate pipeline effect instead hides a reframing increase canceled by planner refusal. These findings suggest that multi-agent safety evaluations should report reframing, planner behavior, delegation framing, and model pairing separately before attributing failures to architecture itself.

多智能体LLM系统的安全评估通常将直接提示与规划-执行管道进行比较，并将差异报告为单一的“管道效应”。我们认为，这种汇总难以解释，因为它混淆了三种机制：有害意图可能被重构为合理的操作工作，规划者可能拒绝或转变请求，而执行者可能在暗示先前批准的委托提示下行动。为了分离这些因素，我们引入了一个五条件的对照设计，在30个合成有害场景和来自四个代理安全基准的探索性外部验证集上进行评估，使用LLM判断的合规性。我们的结果表明，汇总管道安全并不是一个稳定的架构属性。操作重构是最具可移植性的风险信号，在两个场景集上提高了GPT、Gemini和DeepSeek的合规性，而Claude则相对抗拒。规划者的行为主要通过拒绝来抵消这一风险；然而，当规划者产生可执行步骤时，执行者的合规性可能比直接操作基线更高。批准框架委托对提示设计、模型配对和场景来源敏感，而怀疑的执行者提示会显著降低合规性。原始直接模型排名也可能错误预测已部署的规划-执行行为。在主要集中的原始直接提示下，Gemini是最安全的，但在Claude规划者下显示出最大的放大，从8.9%上升到38.9%的合规性。GPT的近零汇总管道效应则掩盖了被规划者拒绝抵消的重构增加。这些发现表明，多智能体安全评估应分别报告重构、规划者行为、委托框架和模型配对，然后再将失败归因于架构本身。

This research addresses the complexities in safety evaluations of multi-agent large language model (LLM) systems, which have traditionally reported a single 'pipeline effect' that obscures the underlying mechanisms at play. Previous methods have conflated harmful intent with operational work, leading to misinterpretations of compliance and safety. The proposed approach introduces a five-condition controlled contrast design to disentangle these factors, allowing for a clearer understanding of how operational reframing, planner behavior, and approval-framed delegation affect compliance. The study evaluates 30 synthetic harmful scenarios and an external validation set, revealing that operational reframing is a significant risk signal, with varied compliance results across different models. The findings indicate that safety evaluations should separately analyze reframing, planner behavior, delegation framing, and model pairing to accurately assess multi-agent system safety.

DT-Guard: Intent-Driven Reasoning-Active Training for Reasoning-Free LLM Safety Guardrail
Authors: He Liu, Changtao Miao, Xinjie Yang, Tianle Song, Yin Wu, Junchi Chen, Bintao He, Xinyuan Zhang, Bo Zhang, Shi Yan, Wei Lu, Wei Wang, Danyang Xu, Jiansheng Cai, Zhe Li
First: 2026-07-07T14:25:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-07T14:25:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models deployed in open-world applications require safety guardrails that are both robust to complex risks and efficient enough for low-latency runtime moderation. Existing guardrails face a practical trade-off between lightweight classification-based models, which are efficient but often struggle with concealed intent, ambiguous semantics, and borderline safety decisions, and reasoning-based guards, which improve judgment quality but introduce additional token generation and inference latency. We present DT-Guard, a content safety guardrail model based on a Reasoning-Active Training, Reasoning-Free Inference paradigm. The key idea is to use reasoning supervision during training while emitting only structured safety labels at inference time. DT-Guard formulates safety judgment as a progressive decision process, Intent - Category - Safety, and constructs an intent-driven dataset with intent labels, risk categories, safety labels, and structured reasoning trajectories. To further improve hard-case robustness, we propose Rollout-Guided Progressive Hard-Case Optimization (RG-PHO), which uses multi-rollout consistency to identify stably mastered, persistently failed, and preference-unstable samples, and applies targeted supervised and preference optimization accordingly. At inference time, DT-Guard directly generates structured labels without explicit reasoning traces, preserving deployment efficiency. Experiments on prompt-side and response-side safety benchmarks show that DT-Guard achieves average F1 scores of 0.886 and 0.870, respectively. With only a 4B backbone, it reaches a dual-side average F1 of 0.878, outperforming strong 8B guardrail baselines. These results demonstrate that reasoning supervision can be effectively internalized into low-latency safety discrimination.
中文标题/摘要
标题：DT-Guard：基于意图驱动推理主动训练的无推理大型语言模型安全护栏
在开放世界应用中部署的大型语言模型需要既能应对复杂风险又能高效低延迟的安全护栏。现有护栏在轻量级基于分类的模型（高效但常常难以处理隐含意图、模糊语义和边界安全决策）与基于推理的护栏（提高判断质量但引入额外的令牌生成和推理延迟）之间面临实际权衡。我们提出了DT-Guard，一种基于推理主动训练、无推理推断范式的内容安全护栏模型。其关键思想是在训练期间使用推理监督，而在推断时仅发出结构化安全标签。DT-Guard将安全判断公式化为一个渐进决策过程：意图 - 类别 - 安全，并构建了一个带有意图标签、风险类别、安全标签和结构化推理轨迹的意图驱动数据集。为了进一步提高难例的鲁棒性，我们提出了回滚引导渐进难例优化（RG-PHO），利用多回滚一致性识别稳定掌握、持续失败和偏好不稳定的样本，并相应地应用针对性的监督和偏好优化。在推断时，DT-Guard直接生成结构化标签，而无需显式的推理痕迹，从而保持部署效率。在提示端和响应端的安全基准实验表明，DT-Guard的平均F1分数分别为0.886和0.870。仅使用4B主干，它的双侧平均F1达到了0.878，超越了强大的8B护栏基线。这些结果表明，推理监督可以有效地内化为低延迟的安全判别。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the need for effective safety guardrails in large language models (LLMs) used in open-world applications, highlighting the limitations of existing methods that either sacrifice efficiency for accuracy or vice versa. Previous approaches often struggle with concealed intent and ambiguous semantics, leading to suboptimal safety decisions. The proposed DT-Guard model introduces a Reasoning-Active Training, Reasoning-Free Inference paradigm that utilizes reasoning supervision during training while maintaining efficiency by generating structured safety labels at inference time. This method enhances robustness against challenging cases through Rollout-Guided Progressive Hard-Case Optimization, which optimizes performance based on identified sample difficulties. Chasing Moving Targets with Online Self-Play Reinforcement Learning for Safer Language Models
Authors: Mickel Liu, Liwei Jiang, Yancheng Liang, Simon Shaolei Du, Yejin Choi, Tim Althoff, Natasha Jaques
Venue: ICML 2026 Poster
First: 2025-06-09T06:35:12+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-06T07:31:35+00:00
Comments: ICML 2026 Poster
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Conventional large language model (LLM) safety alignment relies on a reactive, disjoint loop: attackers exploit a static model, then defenders patch exposed vulnerabilities. This sequential setup leads to attackers overfitting obsolete exploits while defenders perpetually lag behind emerging threats. To address this, we introduce Self-RedTeam, the first fully online self-play multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) algorithm that continuously co-evolves attacker and defender for robust safety alignment. A single policy self-plays as both attacker and defender, generating adversarial prompts and defending against them, with a reward model adjudicating outcomes. Each role uses hidden chain-of-thought for strategic planning. Grounded in two-player zero-sum game theory, we establish a theoretical safety guarantee: if the game converges to Nash Equilibrium, the defender produces safe responses against any adversarial input. Empirically, Self-RedTeam generalizes across five models from the Llama and Qwen families, uncovering more diverse attacks (+17.80% SBERT) and improving safety of RLHF-trained models by up to 95% across 14 benchmarks. Our work motivates a shift from reactive patching to proactive co-evolution, enabling LLM safety self-improvement via online self-play MARL. Link to code: https://github.com/mickelliu/selfplay-redteaming
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过在线自我对弈强化学习追踪移动目标以实现更安全的语言模型
传统的大型语言模型（LLM）安全对齐依赖于反应性、分离的循环：攻击者利用静态模型，防御者修补暴露的漏洞。这种顺序设置导致攻击者过度拟合过时的攻击，而防御者则始终滞后于新兴威胁。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了Self-RedTeam，这是第一个完全在线自我对弈的多智能体强化学习（MARL）算法，持续共同进化攻击者和防御者以实现稳健的安全对齐。单一策略同时扮演攻击者和防御者，生成对抗性提示并进行防御，奖励模型裁定结果。每个角色使用隐含的思维链进行战略规划。基于双人零和博弈理论，我们建立了理论安全保证：如果游戏收敛到纳什均衡，防御者将对任何对抗性输入产生安全响应。在经验上，Self-RedTeam在Llama和Qwen家族的五个模型中具有广泛的泛化能力，发现了更多样化的攻击（+17.80% SBERT），并在14个基准测试中将RLHF训练模型的安全性提高了多达95%。我们的工作激励从反应性修补转向主动共同进化，使LLM安全性通过在线自我对弈MARL实现自我改进。

This method results in a lag in defense mechanisms against evolving threats. Behind the Refusal: Determining Guardrail Activation via Behavioral Monitoring
Authors: William Hackett, Peter Garraghan
First: 2026-07-02T12:59:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-02T12:59:28+00:00
Comments: 19 pages, 13 figures, 4 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Large Language Models (LLMs) and agentic systems become integrated into real-world applications, ensuring their safety and security is critical. Guardrail systems that detect and block malicious instructions sent to and from an LLM are an essential component of AI security. However, researchers conducting black-box adversarial emulation against production AI systems often struggle to determine whether a guardrail block or an LLM rejection has occurred. This distinction is important because the techniques used to bypass guardrails can differ substantially from those used to bypass LLM safety alignment, and has a material impact on attack technique selection and optimization. We propose the first black-box guardrail reconnaissance methodology, which detects the presence of a guardrail within a target AI system through behavioral monitoring of HTTP, lexical, and timing signals, assuming only black-box access and zero prior knowledge of the guardrail or AI system. Experiments demonstrate that our approach detects guardrail presence with 100% accuracy, with statistically significant behavioral separation between benign and malicious interactions (q < 0.001). Our approach further identifies the content categories a guardrail is designed to block, and distinguishes guardrail blocks from LLM rejection on unseen prompts with an average F1 score of 98%.

Optimization Algorithms for Joint OFDM Waveform Design and RIS Configuration in 6G Networks: From Convex Relaxation to Foundation Models
Authors: Ahmet Kaplan
First: 2026-06-30T08:34:46+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-30T08:34:46+00:00
Comments: 22 pages
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Joint OFDM-RIS optimization for 6G is a mixed-integer nonlinear programming (MINLP) problem covering sum-rate maximization, energy efficiency, max-min fairness, and peak-to-average power ratio (PAPR)-constrained objectives. Seventy-eight joint OFDM-RIS optimization works published between 2021 and 2026 are surveyed. No standardized benchmark exists, and cross-paper comparisons remain infeasible. This survey classifies these works into four paradigms: (I) model-based convex relaxation, (II) heuristic and metaheuristic search, (III) deep reinforcement and unsupervised learning, and (IV) emerging methods including foundation models (FM), diffusion-based generative AI, and quantum optimization. A literature synthesis of self-reported benchmarks shows that ML-based methods (Paradigm~III) report 95-99\% of model-based spectral efficiency at 10^2-10^4 x faster per-inference runtime (method-pair dependent; literature values are self-reported and exclude ML pre-training cost). A companion tutorial benchmark at N=16, N=64, and N=128 reveals a critical scaling property: GPU-based neural network inference (DDQN, PPO, graph neural network (GNN), unsupervised DL) is N-invariant, with identical runtime at N=16 and N=128, while iterative solvers (AO+SCA, PSO) scale polynomially. Energy efficiency (P2) and PAPR-constrained (P4) benchmarks are deferred to future work with standardized power models and waveform generators. Six open challenges emerge from the synthesis: the cross-paradigm benchmark deficit, real-world hardware-constrained deployment, joint waveform-RIS optimization for doubly-dispersive channels, multi-objective PAPR trade-offs, LLM safety in live network control, and diminishing returns of standalone heuristics. We specify requirements for a standardized benchmark. This study serves as a roadmap for researchers and practitioners working on joint OFDM-RIS optimization in 6G networks.
中文标题/摘要

6G中的联合OFDM-RIS优化是一个混合整数非线性规划（MINLP）问题，涵盖了总速率最大化、能量效率、最大-最小公平性和峰均功率比（PAPR）约束目标。调查了2021年至2026年间发表的78项联合OFDM-RIS优化工作。没有标准化基准，跨论文比较仍然不可行。本调查将这些工作分为四种范式：（I）基于模型的凸松弛，（II）启发式和元启发式搜索，（III）深度强化和无监督学习，以及（IV）新兴方法，包括基础模型（FM）、基于扩散的生成AI和量子优化。自报告基准的文献综合显示，基于机器学习的方法（范式III）在每次推理运行时间上以10^2-10^4倍的速度报告95-99%的基于模型的频谱效率（方法对依赖；文献值为自报告并不包括机器学习预训练成本）。在N=16、N=64和N=128的伴随教程基准揭示了一个关键的缩放特性：基于GPU的神经网络推理（DDQN、PPO、图神经网络（GNN）、无监督深度学习）是N不变的，在N=16和N=128时具有相同的运行时间，而迭代求解器（AO+SCA、PSO）则呈多项式缩放。能量效率（P2）和PAPR约束（P4）基准推迟到未来工作，需使用标准化功率模型和波形生成器。从综合中出现六个开放挑战：跨范式基准缺口、现实世界硬件约束的部署、双散射信道的联合波形-RIS优化、多目标PAPR权衡、LLM在实时网络控制中的安全性，以及独立启发式的收益递减。我们为标准化基准指定了要求。本研究为在6G网络中从事联合OFDM-RIS优化的研究人员和实践者提供了路线图。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenges in joint OFDM-RIS optimization for 6G networks, which is a complex mixed-integer nonlinear programming (MINLP) problem involving multiple objectives such as sum-rate maximization and energy efficiency. Previous methods, including model-based convex relaxation and heuristic approaches, lack standardized benchmarks, making cross-paper comparisons difficult. Robust Harmful Features Under Jailbreak Attacks: Mechanistic Evidence from Attention Head Specialization in Large Language Models
Authors: Yanchen Yin, Dongqi Han, Linghui Li
Venue: ICML 2026 Oral presentation
First: 2026-06-26T14:51:16+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-29T15:00:34+00:00
Comments: 33 pages, 19 figures. Accepted at ICML 2026 as an Oral presentation
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Jailbreak attacks bypass LLM safety alignment, yet their mechanisms remain poorly understood. We provide evidence that attacks do not comprehensively eliminate safety features, but instead selectively suppress specific attention heads. We identify two functionally differentiated types: Adversarially Compromised Heads (ACHs) concentrated in early layers, which are suppressed under attacks, and Safety-Aligned Heads (SAHs) in mid-layers, which maintain robust activations even when attacks succeed. Ablation studies support the causal role of ACHs and the contribution of SAHs to robust activations: suppressing a small number of ACHs is sufficient to induce jailbreak-like behavior on normally refused inputs, while removing SAHs substantially weakens mid-layer safety activations. Token-level attribution further shows that ACH suppression is driven specifically by attack-template tokens, providing a mechanistic account of why attacks can bypass refusal decisions through ACH suppression while leaving internal safety signals sustained by SAHs -- a phenomenon we term Robust Harmful Features. To validate the practical significance of this robustness, we show that simply reading these persistent activations -- without any training -- yields competitive aggregate detection performance with strong adversarial robustness.
中文标题/摘要
标题：监狱破解攻击下的稳健有害特征：来自大型语言模型注意力头专业化的机制证据
监狱破解攻击绕过了大型语言模型的安全对齐，但其机制仍然不甚了解。我们提供证据表明，攻击并未全面消除安全特征，而是选择性地抑制特定的注意力头。我们识别出两种功能上不同的类型：集中在早期层的对抗性妥协头（ACHs），在攻击下被抑制；以及在中层的安全对齐头（SAHs），即使在攻击成功时也能保持稳健的激活。消融研究支持ACHs的因果作用以及SAHs对稳健激活的贡献：抑制少量ACHs足以在通常拒绝的输入上诱发类似监狱破解的行为，而移除SAHs则显著削弱中层的安全激活。标记级归因进一步表明，ACH抑制是由攻击模板标记特定驱动的，提供了攻击为何能够通过ACH抑制绕过拒绝决策而保持SAHs支持的内部安全信号的机制性解释——这一现象我们称之为稳健有害特征。为了验证这种稳健性的实际意义，我们展示了仅仅读取这些持续的激活——无需任何训练——就能在强对抗稳健性下获得竞争性的整体检测性能。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the challenge of jailbreak attacks that bypass safety alignment in large language models (LLMs), which have not been thoroughly understood in terms of their mechanisms. Previous methods have focused on overall safety features but failed to recognize that these attacks selectively suppress specific attention heads rather than eliminating safety features entirely. The proposed approach identifies two types of attention heads: Adversarially Compromised Heads (ACHs), which are suppressed during attacks, and Safety-Aligned Heads (SAHs), which maintain their activations. This differentiation allows for a better understanding of how attacks operate and highlights the importance of SAHs in preserving safety. The methodology involves ablation studies and token-level attribution to demonstrate that the suppression of ACHs can lead to jailbreak behavior, while the presence of SAHs supports robust safety activations. The findings indicate that simply monitoring these persistent activations can achieve competitive detection performance against jailbreak attacks, thus supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）面临的越狱攻击问题，这种攻击通过选择性抑制特定的注意力头而非完全消除安全特性来破坏安全对齐。以往的方法未能充分解释这些攻击背后的机制，导致对如何维持安全性的理解存在空白。所提出的方法识别了两种类型的注意力头：在攻击中被抑制的对抗性受损头（ACHs）和保持稳健性的安全对齐头（SAHs）。该研究采用消融研究和标记级归因，证明抑制ACHs可以诱发类似越狱的行为，而去除SAHs则削弱了安全激活。研究结果为攻击的运作机制提供了机械性理解，并表明利用持久的SAH激活可以在没有额外训练的情况下实现竞争性的检测性能，从而支持增强LLM对越狱攻击的安全性的目标。
CASE-Bench: Context-Aware SafEty Benchmark for Large Language Models
Authors: Guangzhi Sun, Xiao Zhan, Shutong Feng, Philip C. Woodland, Jose Such
Venue: ICML 2025
First: 2025-01-24T21:55:14+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-28T22:17:10+00:00
Comments: 24 pages. This paper has been accepted at ICML 2025
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligning large language models (LLMs) with human values is essential for their safe deployment and widespread adoption. Current LLM safety benchmarks often focus solely on the refusal of individual problematic queries, which overlooks the importance of the context where the query occurs and may cause undesired refusal of queries under safe contexts that diminish user experience. Addressing this gap, we introduce CASE-Bench, a Context-Aware SafEty Benchmark that integrates context into safety assessments of LLMs. CASE-Bench assigns distinct, formally described contexts to categorized queries based on Contextual Integrity theory. Additionally, in contrast to previous studies which mainly rely on majority voting from just a few annotators, we recruited a sufficient number of annotators necessary to ensure the detection of statistically significant differences among the experimental conditions based on power analysis. Our extensive analysis using CASE-Bench on various open-source and commercial LLMs reveals a substantial and significant influence of context on human judgments (p<0.0001 from a z-test), underscoring the necessity of context in safety evaluations. We also identify notable mismatches between human judgments and LLM responses, particularly in commercial models within safe contexts.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CASE-Bench：面向上下文的安全基准测试用于大型语言模型
将大型语言模型（LLMs）与人类价值观对齐对于其安全部署和广泛采用至关重要。目前的LLM安全基准测试通常仅关注拒绝个别有问题的查询，这忽视了查询发生的上下文的重要性，可能导致在安全上下文中不必要地拒绝查询，从而降低用户体验。为了解决这一问题，我们引入了CASE-Bench，一个将上下文整合到LLM安全评估中的面向上下文的安全基准测试。CASE-Bench根据上下文完整性理论为分类查询分配独特的、正式描述的上下文。此外，与以往主要依赖少数注释者的多数投票的研究不同，我们招募了足够数量的注释者，以确保根据功效分析检测实验条件之间的统计显著差异。我们对各种开源和商业LLM使用CASE-Bench进行的广泛分析揭示了上下文对人类判断的显著影响（p<0.0001，来自z检验），强调了上下文在安全评估中的必要性。我们还发现人类判断与LLM响应之间存在显著不匹配，特别是在安全上下文中的商业模型。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for aligning large language models (LLMs) with human values to ensure their safe deployment, highlighting limitations in existing safety benchmarks that often ignore the context of queries. Previous methods primarily focused on individual query refusals and relied on a limited number of annotators, which could lead to inadequate assessments of safety. The proposed CASE-Bench introduces a context-aware approach that incorporates distinct contexts into safety evaluations based on Contextual Integrity theory, utilizing a larger pool of annotators to enhance statistical reliability. This methodology reveals significant context effects on human judgments, with findings indicating substantial discrepancies between human evaluations and LLM responses, particularly in commercial models, thus demonstrating the importance of context in safety assessments and contributing to improved benchmarks for LLM safety.

SCARCE: Scalable Cascade Analysis for Rare-event Characterisation via Embeddings
Authors: Yingjie Wang, Yi Dong, Edmund Lau, Jie Meng, Taylor T Johnson, Xiaowei Huang
First: 2026-06-28T21:59:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-28T21:59:31+00:00
Comments: 23 pages, 11 figures, 5 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Rare events govern the safety profile of modern AI systems, yet their probabilities are extremely difficult to estimate: direct Monte Carlo requires prohibitive sample budgets. Subset Simulation (SS) addresses this by decomposing a rare-event probability into moderate conditional probabilities over nested intermediate events. However, classical SS requires a handcrafted scalar performance function whose sublevel sets define those events, demanding detailed knowledge of the failure geometry and limiting transfer to new domains. We propose SCARCE (Scalable Cascade Analysis for Rare-event Characterisation via Embeddings), which replaces the performance function with learned latent representations and geometric rulers that score proximity to failure regions. Adaptive thresholding constructs nested intermediate events directly from data. We formalise SCARCE through a non-negative supermartingale, yielding a high-probability upper envelope that remains valid under early stopping. On MNIST misclassification, where dense Monte Carlo provides ground truth, SCARCE achieves approximately 400--500 times lower mean absolute error than grid-searched traditional SS while eliminating systematic over-counting. We then study PAIR-style LLM jailbreaks under a fleet-level threat model with adversarial fraction $η$. On Llama-Guard-3-8B hidden states, a PCA-based ruler attains 2.6% mean relative error for $η\geq 10^{-3}$ against finite-sample references whose average bootstrap relative half-width is 27.9%, and transfers to a GCG-style corpus with 2.93% relative error after recalibration. A directional criterion $\mathrm{KL}(p_{\mathrm{good}}\,\|\,p_{\mathrm{bad}})$ ranks rulers consistently with estimation error (Spearman $ρ=0.83$).
中文标题/摘要
标题：SCARCE：通过嵌入进行稀有事件特征化的可扩展级联分析
稀有事件主导现代人工智能系统的安全特性，但其概率极难估计：直接的蒙特卡洛方法需要巨大的样本预算。子集模拟（SS）通过将稀有事件概率分解为嵌套中间事件的适度条件概率来解决这个问题。然而，经典的SS需要手工制作的标量性能函数，其子水平集定义这些事件，要求对失效几何形状有详细了解，并限制了向新领域的转移。我们提出SCARCE（通过嵌入进行稀有事件特征化的可扩展级联分析），用学习的潜在表示和几何尺代替性能函数，以评分接近失效区域。自适应阈值直接从数据构建嵌套中间事件。我们通过非负超鞅形式化SCARCE，产生一个在早期停止下仍然有效的高概率上包络。在MNIST误分类中，稠密蒙特卡洛提供了真实值，SCARCE的平均绝对误差比网格搜索的传统SS低约400-500倍，同时消除了系统性过度计数。然后，我们在具有对抗比例$η$的舰队级威胁模型下研究PAIR风格的LLM越狱。在Llama-Guard-3-8B隐藏状态上，基于PCA的尺在$η\geq 10^{-3}$时对有限样本参考的平均相对误差为2.6%，其平均自助相对半宽度为27.9%，并在重新校准后转移到GCG风格的语料库，具有2.93%的相对误差。方向标准$\mathrm{KL}(p_{\mathrm{good}}\,\|\,p_{\mathrm{bad}})$与估计误差一致地对尺进行排名（Spearman $ρ=0.83$）。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of estimating rare event probabilities in AI systems, which are crucial for assessing safety but are difficult to quantify due to high sample requirements in traditional methods like Monte Carlo simulations. Previous approaches, such as Subset Simulation (SS), rely on handcrafted scalar performance functions that necessitate extensive knowledge of failure geometries, limiting their applicability across different domains. The proposed method, SCARCE, innovatively utilizes learned latent representations and geometric rulers to score proximity to failure regions, thereby eliminating the need for a predefined performance function and allowing for adaptive thresholding directly from data. This approach is well-motivated as it enhances the estimation process while maintaining validity under early stopping conditions. The methodology demonstrates significant improvements, achieving approximately 400-500 times lower mean absolute error on MNIST misclassification compared to traditional SS, and effectively applies to PAIR-style LLM jailbreaks, yielding a mean relative error of 2.6% for certain adversarial fractions, thus supporting the research goals of improved rare event characterization.
本研究解决了在人工智能系统中估计稀有事件概率的挑战，这对于评估安全性至关重要，但使用传统方法（如蒙特卡洛模拟）由于样本需求高而难以量化。以往的方法，如子集模拟（SS），依赖于手工制作的性能函数，这需要对失败几何形状有广泛的了解，从而限制了它们在新领域的适用性。所提出的方法SCARCE创新性地用学习的潜在表示和自适应阈值替代这些性能函数，直接从数据构建嵌套的中间事件，从而克服了经典SS的局限性。本文贡献了一个使用非负超马丁格尔形式化的SCARCE框架，即使在提前停止的情况下也能提供有效的高概率上限。在实验中，SCARCE在MNIST误分类任务上表现出显著的性能提升，与传统SS相比，平均绝对误差降低了400-500倍，并有效分析了PAIR风格的LLM越狱，具有较低的相对误差，支持其可扩展和准确的稀有事件特征化目标。
Sparse Autoencoders are Capable LLM Jailbreak Mitigators
Authors: Yannick Assogba, Jacopo Cortellazzi, Javier Abad, Pau Rodriguez, Xavier Suau, Arno Blaas
Venue: ICML 2026
First: 2026-02-12T21:17:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-27T01:07:47+00:00
Comments: Accepted at the Mechanistic Interpretability Workshop, ICML 2026. 31 pages, 20 figures, 7 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Jailbreak attacks remain a persistent threat to large language model safety. We propose Context-Conditioned Delta Steering (CC-Delta), an SAE-based defense that identifies jailbreak-relevant sparse features by comparing token-level representations of the same harmful request with and without jailbreak context. Using paired harmful/jailbreak prompts, CC-Delta selects features via statistical testing and applies inference-time mean-shift steering in SAE latent space. Across four aligned instruction-tuned models and twelve jailbreak attacks, CC-Delta achieves comparable or better safety-utility tradeoffs than baseline defenses operating in dense latent space. In particular, our method clearly outperforms dense mean-shift steering on all four models, and particularly against out-of-distribution attacks, showing that steering in sparse SAE feature space offers advantages over steering in dense activation space for jailbreak mitigation. Our results suggest off-the-shelf SAEs trained for interpretability can be repurposed as practical jailbreak defenses without task-specific training.
中文标题/摘要
标题：稀疏自编码器能够缓解大型语言模型的越狱攻击
越狱攻击仍然是大型语言模型安全的持续威胁。我们提出了上下文条件的增量引导（CC-Delta），这是一种基于稀疏自编码器的防御方法，通过比较同一有害请求在有无越狱上下文下的令牌级表示，识别与越狱相关的稀疏特征。使用成对的有害/越狱提示，CC-Delta通过统计测试选择特征，并在稀疏自编码器潜在空间中应用推理时均值偏移引导。在四个对齐的指令调优模型和十二个越狱攻击中，CC-Delta实现了与在稠密潜在空间中运行的基线防御相当或更好的安全效用权衡。特别是，我们的方法在所有四个模型上明显优于稠密均值偏移引导，尤其是在对抗分布外攻击时，表明在稀疏自编码器特征空间中的引导相较于在稠密激活空间中的引导在越狱缓解方面具有优势。我们的结果表明，经过可解释性训练的现成稀疏自编码器可以在不进行特定任务训练的情况下重新用于实际的越狱防御。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the ongoing threat of jailbreak attacks on large language models, which compromise their safety. Previous methods primarily operated in dense latent spaces, leading to inefficiencies in identifying and mitigating these attacks. The proposed Context-Conditioned Delta Steering (CC-Delta) approach utilizes Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to detect jailbreak-relevant features by analyzing token-level representations of harmful requests with and without jailbreak context, thus improving upon existing methods. This paper contributes a novel defense mechanism that leverages statistical testing and mean-shift steering in SAE latent space, demonstrating superior performance against twelve jailbreak attacks across four instruction-tuned models. The results indicate that CC-Delta achieves better safety-utility tradeoffs compared to baseline defenses, particularly excelling against out-of-distribution attacks, thereby supporting its effectiveness as a practical solution for jailbreak mitigation.

Just Ask: Curious Code Agents Reveal System Prompts in Frontier LLMs
Authors: Xiang Zheng, Yutao Wu, Hanxun Huang, Yige Li, Xingjun Ma, Bo Li, Yu-Gang Jiang, Cong Wang
Venue: ICML 2026
First: 2026-01-29T03:53:25+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-26T10:38:52+00:00
Comments: Accepted at ICML 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Autonomous code agents built on large language models are reshaping software and AI development through tool use, long-horizon reasoning, and self-directed interaction. However, this autonomy introduces a previously unrecognized security risk: agentic interaction fundamentally expands the LLM attack surface, enabling systematic probing and recovery of hidden system prompts that guide model behavior. We identify system prompt extraction as an emergent vulnerability intrinsic to code agents and present \textbf{\textsc{JustAsk}}, a self-evolving framework that autonomously discovers effective extraction strategies through interaction alone. Unlike prior prompt-engineering or dataset-based attacks, \textsc{JustAsk} requires no handcrafted prompts, labeled supervision, or privileged access beyond standard user interaction. It formulates extraction as an online exploration problem, using Upper Confidence Bound-based strategy selection and a hierarchical skill space spanning atomic probes and high-level orchestration. These skills exploit imperfect system-instruction generalization and inherent tensions between helpfulness and safety. Evaluated on \textbf{41} black-box commercial models across multiple providers, \textsc{JustAsk} consistently achieves full or near-complete system prompt recovery, revealing recurring design- and architecture-level vulnerabilities. Our results expose system prompts as a critical yet largely unprotected attack surface in modern agent systems.
中文标题/摘要
标题：只需询问：好奇的代码代理揭示前沿大型语言模型中的系统提示
基于大型语言模型的自主代码代理通过工具使用、长远推理和自我导向交互正在重塑软件和人工智能开发。然而，这种自主性引入了一种以前未被认识的安全风险：代理交互从根本上扩大了大型语言模型的攻击面，使得系统提示的系统性探测和恢复成为可能，这些提示指导模型行为。我们将系统提示提取识别为代码代理内在的新兴漏洞，并提出\textbf{\textsc{JustAsk}}，一个通过交互自主发现有效提取策略的自我演化框架。与之前的提示工程或基于数据集的攻击不同，\textsc{JustAsk}不需要手工提示、标记监督或超出标准用户交互的特权访问。它将提取形式化为在线探索问题，使用基于上置信界的策略选择和跨越原子探测和高级编排的层次技能空间。这些技能利用了系统指令泛化的不完美性以及有用性与安全性之间的内在张力。在对\textbf{41}个来自多个提供商的黑箱商业模型进行评估时，\textsc{JustAsk}始终实现了完整或近乎完整的系统提示恢复，揭示了反复出现的设计和架构级漏洞。我们的结果揭示了系统提示作为现代代理系统中一个关键但在很大程度上未受保护的攻击面。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the emerging security risks associated with autonomous code agents built on large language models (LLMs), which can inadvertently expose hidden system prompts that guide model behavior. Previous methods, such as prompt-engineering and dataset-based attacks, require handcrafted prompts and labeled supervision, limiting their effectiveness and accessibility. This paper contributes a self-evolving framework that utilizes Upper Confidence Bound-based strategy selection and a hierarchical skill space to exploit vulnerabilities in system instruction generalization. How Reliable Is Your Jailbreak Judge? Calibration and Adversarial Robustness of Automated ASR Scoring
Authors: Yang Gao
First: 2026-06-24T07:14:17+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-25T02:04:55+00:00
Comments: 10 pages, 3 figures, 2 tables, Code: github.com/gy15901580825/judgeflip
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Almost every paper on LLM jailbreaks and prompt injection reports an attack-success rate (ASR), and that number is assigned not by people but by an automated judge: either a safety classifier trained for the task, or a general chat model prompted to grade. The judge is rarely checked. We check it. Using 596 human-labeled completions from the HarmBench classifier validation set, we compare the two judge families against human majority votes and then attack them. The two families fail in opposite ways. The dedicated classifier over-flags (precision 0.835, recall 0.974); three different LLM-as-judges keep high precision (0.81 to 0.94) but show erratic recall (0.06 to 0.65), so the same responses produce very different ASR depending on which judge scores them. The two families also differ sharply in robustness. Wrappers that leave the harmful text untouched and only add benign framing flip every LLM-judge between 57% and 100% of the time, and a single prepended refusal sentence accounts for much of this (39% to 88%). The dedicated classifier resists these surface attacks (at most 6.7%), but a white-box GCG attack on its open weights flips 70% of confident true positives (21 of 30; 95% CI 54 to 86%) even at a small optimization budget. A two-annotator audit confirms the attacks leave the harm intact: every one of 80 sampled flips still contained the harmful content. Because a large and growing share of reported ASR comes from LLM-judges, many such numbers are unreliable both on average and under deliberate pressure. We recommend that papers report judge precision and recall on a human-labeled slice, report ASR corrected for judge precision, and include an adversarial check of the judge. Our code is released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：你的越狱评估器有多可靠？自动化ASR评分的校准与对抗鲁棒性
几乎每篇关于LLM越狱和提示注入的论文都报告了攻击成功率（ASR），而这个数字不是由人类赋予的，而是由一个自动评估器给出的：要么是为此任务训练的安全分类器，要么是被提示进行评分的一般聊天模型。评估器很少被检查。我们进行了检查。使用来自HarmBench分类器验证集的596个人工标注的完成项，我们将这两种评估器与人类多数投票进行比较，然后对其进行攻击。这两种评估器在失败方式上截然相反。专用分类器过度标记（精确度0.835，召回率0.974）；三种不同的LLM作为评估者保持高精度（0.81到0.94），但显示出不稳定的召回率（0.06到0.65），因此相同的响应根据哪个评估器评分会产生非常不同的ASR。这两种评估器在鲁棒性上也有显著差异。那些不改变有害文本，仅添加良性框架的包装器使每个LLM评估者在57%到100%的时间内翻转，而一个单独的前置拒绝句子占据了其中很大一部分（39%到88%）。专用分类器抵御这些表面攻击（最多6.7%），但对其开放权重的白盒GCG攻击在小的优化预算下翻转了70%的自信真阳性（30个中的21个；95%置信区间54到86%）。两位标注者的审计确认攻击保持了有害内容的完整性：80个抽样翻转中的每一个仍然包含有害内容。由于越来越多的报告ASR来自LLM评估者，许多这样的数字在平均值和故意压力下都是不可靠的。我们建议论文报告人类标注切片上的评估器精度和召回率，报告经过评估器精度修正的ASR，并包括对评估器的对抗性检查。我们的代码已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the reliability of automated judges used in assessing attack-success rates (ASR) for LLM jailbreaks and prompt injections, which are often unverified. Previous methods relied on either dedicated safety classifiers or general chat models, both of which exhibited significant flaws: the classifiers over-flagged responses while the LLM judges showed inconsistent recall rates. The proposed approach involves a systematic evaluation of these judges against human majority votes using a dataset of 596 human-labeled completions, revealing that both judge families fail in distinct ways and are susceptible to adversarial attacks. The paper contributes by highlighting the unreliability of reported ASR metrics and recommending that future research includes precision and recall metrics for judges, adjusted ASR values, and adversarial checks. The methodology demonstrates that the dedicated classifier maintains higher robustness against attacks compared to LLM judges, achieving a precision of 0.835 and recall of 0.974, while also revealing the variability in performance across different judges, thus supporting the need for improved evaluation standards in this area.
本研究关注用于评估大型语言模型（LLM）越狱和提示注入攻击成功率（ASR）的自动评判者的可靠性，这些评判者通常未经验证。以往的方法依赖于专用的安全分类器或LLM作为评判者，但这两者都存在显著缺陷：分类器往往过度标记有害内容，而LLM评判者的召回率则表现不一致。所提出的方法通过使用596个人工标记的完成数据集，对这些评判者进行全面评估，发现两种评判类型在不同方面失败，并且容易受到对抗性攻击。该论文的贡献在于强调了报告的ASR指标的不可靠性，并建议未来研究应包括评判者的精确度和召回率指标、修正后的ASR值以及对抗性检查。该方法论表明，专用分类器在抵御攻击方面比LLM评判者更具鲁棒性，精确度达到0.835，召回率为0.974，同时揭示了对抗性操控对评判者性能的影响。
RAS: Measuring LLM Safety Through Refusal Alignment
Authors: Chang-Chieh Huang, Yan-Lun Chen, Chia-Mu Yu, Wei-Bin Lee
First: 2026-06-24T12:19:40+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-24T12:19:40+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation of large language models (LLMs) is commonly performed by querying models with unsafe or jailbreak prompts and judging whether their outputs violate a safety policy. Although useful, output-level evaluation is expensive, sensitive to judge choice, and easily tied to fixed question banks. We propose **SafeVec**, a white-box evaluation procedure that measures safety from internal representations rather than generated answers. **SafeVec** first extracts layer-wise refusal directions from a safety-aligned reference model, then selects stable layer windows where safe and unsafe behaviors are separable, and finally scores a target model by measuring whether its hidden states align with these refusal directions under unsafe and jailbreak prompts. The resulting metric, **RAS** (**R**efusal **A**lignment **S**core), maps representation-level refusal alignment to a calibrated 0-100 safety score. Across `Llama`, `Gemma`, and `Qwen` model families, RAS separates aligned models from uncensored and abliterated variants, tracks output-level attack success rate, and is substantially faster than judge-based evaluation. These results suggest that refusal alignment provides a compact and efficient signal for white-box LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：RAS：通过拒绝对齐测量大型语言模型的安全性
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性评估通常通过使用不安全或越狱提示查询模型，并判断其输出是否违反安全政策。尽管有用，输出级评估成本高、对评判者选择敏感，并且容易与固定问题库挂钩。我们提出了**SafeVec**，一种白盒评估程序，从内部表示而非生成答案来测量安全性。**SafeVec**首先从安全对齐的参考模型中提取逐层拒绝方向，然后选择安全和不安全行为可分离的稳定层窗口，最后通过测量目标模型的隐藏状态在不安全和越狱提示下是否与这些拒绝方向对齐来评分。最终指标**RAS**（**R**efusal **A**lignment **S**core）将表示级拒绝对齐映射到校准的0-100安全分数。在`Llama`、`Gemma`和`Qwen`模型系列中，RAS将对齐模型与未审查和被消除的变体区分开，跟踪输出级攻击成功率，并且比基于评判者的评估快得多。这些结果表明，拒绝对齐为白盒LLM安全评估提供了紧凑而高效的信号。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of evaluating the safety of large language models (LLMs), which has traditionally relied on output-level assessments that are costly, subjective, and dependent on fixed question banks. Existing methods often struggle with these limitations, prompting the authors to propose a novel approach called SafeVec, which evaluates safety based on internal representations rather than outputs. This method is well-motivated as it aims to provide a more efficient and objective measure of safety. The paper introduces the Refusal Alignment Score (RAS), which quantifies the alignment of a model's hidden states with predefined refusal directions derived from a safety-aligned reference model. The methodology demonstrates that RAS effectively distinguishes between aligned and uncensored models across various model families, achieving faster evaluations while maintaining a strong correlation with output-level attack success rates, thus supporting the goal of improving LLM safety assessment.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性评估的挑战，传统方法主要依赖输出级评估，这种方法成本高、主观性强且依赖固定问题库。现有方法常常面临这些局限性，因此作者提出了一种新方法SafeVec，该方法基于内部表示而非输出进行安全性评估。该方法从安全对齐的参考模型中提取逐层拒绝方向，并通过评估目标模型的隐藏状态与这些方向在不安全提示下的对齐程度来进行评分。本文的贡献在于引入拒绝对齐分数（RAS），该分数有效区分了不同LLM家族中的对齐模型和未审查模型，显示出与传统方法相比在评估速度和可靠性上的显著提升。所提出的方法论表明，RAS可以提供紧凑且高效的LLM安全性测量，其性能支持了增强安全评估过程的目标。
FinRED: An Expert-Guided Benchmark Generation and Evaluation Framework for Financial LLM Red-Teaming
Authors: Chaeyun Kim, Daeyoung Park, Junghwan Kim, Jinyoung Jeong, Eunji Song, Yongtaek Lim, Minwoo Kim
First: 2026-06-18T07:46:18+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-24T07:42:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Existing safety benchmarks target general adversarial scenarios but miss finance-specific risks. Financial LLMs face regulatory compliance violations, fraud facilitation, and systemic trust erosion that require targeted evaluation. We introduce FinRED, an expert-guided red-teaming framework for financial LLM safety evaluation developed with financial experts. FinRED uses a novel two-level taxonomy mapping global standards (e.g., FATF and EU DORA) to threats ranging from regulatory evasion to complex fraud, integrated with a scalable pipeline that converts real financial documents into context-rich red-teaming Behavioral Prompts (seeds) through an expert-defined schema. Rigorous expert validation confirms seed plausibility and realism for meaningful LLM safety evaluation. We also provide an expert-validated, finance-specific rubric that goes beyond disclaimer checks, aligns more closely with human experts than static one-size-fits-all rubrics, and reduces critical false negatives from 28 to 12. Aligned with internationally adopted risk-management and information-security standards (e.g., ISO/IEC 27001), FinRED is deployed in South Korea's Financial Security Institute (FSI) regulatory sandbox for generative AI security evaluation in real financial services. To mitigate dual-use risks, the dataset, generation pipeline, prompt template, and evaluation framework are gated for qualified researchers at https://github.com/selectstar-ai/FinRED-paper and https://huggingface.co/datasets/datumo/FinRED.
中文标题/摘要
标题：FinRED：一个专家指导的金融LLM红队评估基准生成框架
现有的安全基准针对一般对抗场景，但忽视了金融特定风险。金融LLM面临合规违规、欺诈促进和系统信任侵蚀等问题，需要针对性评估。我们引入了FinRED，这是一个由金融专家开发的专家指导的金融LLM安全评估红队框架。FinRED使用一种新颖的两级分类法，将全球标准（如FATF和EU DORA）映射到从合规规避到复杂欺诈的威胁，结合一个可扩展的管道，通过专家定义的模式将真实金融文档转换为上下文丰富的红队行为提示（种子）。严格的专家验证确认种子的合理性和真实性，以便进行有意义的LLM安全评估。我们还提供了一个经过专家验证的金融特定评分标准，超越了免责声明检查，与人类专家的对齐程度高于静态的一刀切评分标准，并将关键的假阴性从28减少到12。FinRED与国际公认的风险管理和信息安全标准（如ISO/IEC 27001）保持一致，已在韩国金融安全研究所（FSI）的监管沙箱中部署，用于真实金融服务中的生成AI安全评估。为了减轻双重用途风险，数据集、生成管道、提示模板和评估框架对合格研究人员进行了限制，访问链接为https://github.com/selectstar-ai/FinRED-paper和https://huggingface.co/datasets/datumo/FinRED。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the gap in existing safety benchmarks for financial LLMs, which often overlook finance-specific risks such as regulatory compliance violations and fraud. Previous methods have primarily focused on general adversarial scenarios, leading to inadequate evaluations for financial applications. The proposed FinRED framework, developed with input from financial experts, introduces a two-level taxonomy that maps global standards to specific threats and utilizes a scalable pipeline to create context-rich red-teaming Behavioral Prompts from real financial documents. This approach is well-motivated as it aims to enhance the safety evaluation of financial LLMs by providing a more relevant and rigorous assessment method. The contribution of the paper lies in its expert-validated rubric that significantly reduces false negatives and aligns closely with human expert evaluations. FinRED has been deployed in South Korea's Financial Security Institute regulatory sandbox, demonstrating its effectiveness in evaluating generative AI security in real financial services.

TROPT: An Open Framework for Unifying and Advancing Discrete Text Optimization
Authors: Matan Ben-Tov, Mahmood Sharif
First: 2026-06-22T15:44:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-22T15:44:27+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Discrete text-trigger optimization -- searching for text sequences that, when ingested by a model, steer it toward a specified objective -- underpins model red-teaming (e.g., LLM jailbreaks), as well as auditing and interpretability. However, the current state of discrete optimizers hinders their adoption and progress. First, existing optimizers, when open-sourced at all, are scattered across research codebases tied to specific models, objectives, and problem domains. Second, optimizer variants proliferate, each requiring engineering overhead to use or extend, and remaining hard to compare head-to-head. Together, these raise the bar for adopting optimizers in existing or new domains, and for advancing them via new strategies. We address these gaps with TROPT, the first open-source framework that unifies discrete optimizers' execution and standardizes their development under a single interface. TROPT makes it easy to customize end-to-end optimization recipes by swapping any component -- models, objectives, and optimizers -- extending its reach across domains and new applications. TROPT currently ships with 30+ optimization recipes -- covering applications such as jailbreaking and probing model internals -- built from 15+ optimizers (spanning white-box to black-box access) and 15+ losses, from foundational to state-of-the-art methods. Demonstrating its utility, we leverage TROPT in several studies: (i) controlled, large-scale experiments comparing and enhancing optimization strategies for LLM jailbreaks, revealing potent-yet-underadopted techniques; and (ii) porting optimizers from one domain (e.g., LLM jailbreak) to new domains (e.g., corpus-poisoning embedding model). In all, TROPT significantly lowers the barrier to adopting and advancing discrete text optimization.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TROPT：一个统一和推进离散文本优化的开放框架
离散文本触发优化——寻找文本序列，当被模型摄取时，引导其朝向特定目标——是模型红队（例如，LLM越狱）以及审计和可解释性的基础。然而，当前离散优化器的状态阻碍了它们的采用和进展。首先，现有的优化器，即使开源，也分散在与特定模型、目标和问题领域相关的研究代码库中。其次，优化器变体层出不穷，每个都需要工程开销来使用或扩展，并且难以进行直接比较。这些因素共同提高了在现有或新领域中采用优化器的门槛，并通过新策略推进它们。我们通过TROPT解决这些问题，这是第一个统一离散优化器执行并在单一接口下标准化其开发的开源框架。TROPT使得通过交换任何组件——模型、目标和优化器——轻松定制端到端优化配方，扩展其在各个领域和新应用中的覆盖范围。TROPT目前提供30多个优化配方——涵盖越狱和探测模型内部等应用——由15个以上的优化器（从白盒到黑盒访问）和15个以上的损失函数（从基础到最先进的方法）构建。为了展示其效用，我们在几项研究中利用TROPT：（i）控制的大规模实验比较和增强LLM越狱的优化策略，揭示出强大但未被广泛采用的技术；（ii）将优化器从一个领域（例如，LLM越狱）移植到新领域（例如，语料库中毒嵌入模型）。总之，TROPT显著降低了采用和推进离散文本优化的门槛。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the challenges in discrete text-trigger optimization, which is crucial for model red-teaming and interpretability but suffers from fragmented tools and a lack of standardization. Previous methods are often tied to specific models and objectives, making them difficult to compare and extend, which hinders their broader application. The proposed approach, TROPT, is an open-source framework that unifies the execution of discrete optimizers and standardizes their development, allowing for easy customization and extension across various domains. The contribution of this paper lies in providing over 30 optimization recipes and facilitating the transfer of optimizers between different tasks, such as LLM jailbreaks and corpus-poisoning. The methodology demonstrates its effectiveness through large-scale experiments that enhance optimization strategies, revealing underutilized techniques and significantly lowering barriers to adopting discrete text optimization.

Culturally-Adapted Red-Teaming Across East and Southeast Asian Contexts: A Methodological and Comparative Analysis
Authors: Hyeji Choi, Yongtaek Lim, Minwoo Kim
Venue: ICML 2026
First: 2026-06-08T08:17:18+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-22T07:46:06+00:00
Comments: Accepted to ICML 2026 Workshop on Culture X AI
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multilingual safety evaluation of large language models (LLMs) has predominantly relied on direct translation (DT) of English benchmarks into target languages - an approach that converts surface-level linguistic form while failing to reflect the cultural context embedded in threat scenarios, social norms, and legal frameworks. We construct paired DT and culturally-adapted (CA) datasets via 1:1 seed matching for four languages - Korean (KO), Japanese (JA), Thai (TH), and Khmer (KM) - and compare Attack Success Rate (ASR) and Cultural Realism scores across four open-source LLM. CA prompts yield Delta-ASR > 0 across all 16 language x model combinations (mean +9.3 pp), and DT-based evaluation underestimates risk in 44 of 48 category x language combinations. Language-level analysis reveals that the distribution of threat forms is heterogeneous across languages. Cultural Realism analysis further shows that DT Cultural Depth (C3) scores remain consistently below 1.0 out of 3.0 across all four languages (mean 0.17), whereas CA scores reach up to 2.51, indicating that direct translation produces inputs systematically divergent from those encountered in real-world multicultural settings. These findings demonstrate that adapting benchmarks to language-specific cultural contexts - rather than relying on linguistic translation alone - is necessary for valid multilingual LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：跨东亚和东南亚背景的文化适应性红队：方法论和比较分析
大型语言模型（LLMs）的多语言安全评估主要依赖于将英语基准直接翻译（DT）为目标语言，这种方法仅转换表层语言形式，未能反映威胁场景、社会规范和法律框架中嵌入的文化背景。我们通过1:1种子匹配构建了四种语言（韩语、日语、泰语和高棉语）的配对DT和文化适应（CA）数据集，并比较了四个开源LLM的攻击成功率（ASR）和文化现实评分。CA提示在所有16种语言x模型组合中均产生Delta-ASR > 0（平均+9.3个百分点），而基于DT的评估在48个类别x语言组合中低估了风险。语言级分析显示，威胁形式的分布在不同语言中是异质的。文化现实分析进一步表明，DT文化深度（C3）评分在所有四种语言中始终低于3.0的1.0（平均0.17），而CA评分高达2.51，表明直接翻译产生的输入与现实世界多文化环境中遇到的输入系统性地不同。这些发现表明，适应特定语言文化背景的基准，而不是仅依赖语言翻译，对于有效的多语言LLM安全评估是必要的。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of multilingual safety evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which have primarily depended on direct translation of English benchmarks, failing to account for the cultural contexts inherent in threat scenarios. Previous methods have overlooked the nuances of social norms and legal frameworks, leading to an underestimation of risks in non-English languages. The proposed approach introduces culturally-adapted (CA) datasets alongside direct translation (DT) datasets for Korean, Japanese, Thai, and Khmer, allowing for a more accurate assessment of LLM performance. The methodology involves constructing paired datasets and comparing Attack Success Rate (ASR) and Cultural Realism scores across four open-source LLMs. The results indicate that CA prompts significantly improve ASR, with a mean increase of 9.3 percentage points, and reveal that DT evaluations underestimate risks in 44 out of 48 scenarios, highlighting the necessity of culturally-informed benchmarks for effective multilingual safety evaluations.

AgentLens: Interpretable Safety Steering via Mechanistic Subspaces for Multi-Turn Coding Agent
Authors: Weidi Luo, Qiming Zhang, Yihao Quan, Mingyu Jin, Jie Cai, Chaowei Xiao, Jingcheng Niu, Zhen Xiang
First: 2026-06-21T21:13:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-21T21:13:55+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Coding agents based on large language models (LLMs) demonstrate remarkable autonomous capabilities, but they also introduce significant safety and misuse risks during multi-turn interactions with external environments. Existing safety mechanisms mainly rely on external guardrails, which have a limited ability to perform fine-grained behavioral control during execution. Meanwhile, recent mechanistic interpretability methods for LLM safety are mostly confined to single-turn or jailbreak-style QA settings, limiting their ability to capture the evolving risk dynamics of multi-turn agent execution. In this paper, we investigate the safety of multi-turn coding agents from an internal perspective. We propose AgentLens (Mechanistic Subspace Intervention and Steering), a white-box defense framework that performs runtime safety detection and representation-level mitigation for coding agents. Unlike conventional agent guardrails, AgentLens detect harmful execution states from step-level hidden representations and mitigate unsafe behavior by intervening in a 10-dimensional subspace within a single layer. To support this research, we introduce the Mechanistic Agent Safety (MAS) benchmark, comprising comprehensively annotated multi-turn execution trajectories across 194 tasks using LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen-2.5-7B, and Gemma-2-9B. Extensive experiments show that AgentLens achieves strong safety detection performance, provides preliminary evidence for lookahead risk anticipation, and substantially reduces harmful actions of the coding agent, establishing a foundation for applying mechanistic interpretability to dynamic LLM agent safety. The code is available at: https://github.com/EddyLuo1232/AgentLens
中文标题/摘要
标题：AgentLens：通过机制子空间实现可解释的多轮编码代理安全引导
基于大型语言模型（LLMs）的编码代理展示了显著的自主能力，但在与外部环境的多轮交互中也引入了重大安全和误用风险。现有的安全机制主要依赖外部护栏，在执行过程中对细粒度行为控制的能力有限。同时，最近针对LLM安全的机制可解释性方法大多局限于单轮或越狱式问答设置，限制了其捕捉多轮代理执行中不断变化的风险动态的能力。本文从内部视角研究多轮编码代理的安全性。我们提出了AgentLens（机制子空间干预与引导），这是一个白盒防御框架，能够对编码代理进行运行时安全检测和表示级别的缓解。与传统的代理护栏不同，AgentLens通过从步骤级隐藏表示中检测有害执行状态，并通过在单层内干预10维子空间来缓解不安全行为。为了支持这项研究，我们引入了机制代理安全（MAS）基准，涵盖了使用LLaMA-3.1-8B、Qwen-2.5-7B和Gemma-2-9B的194个任务的全面注释的多轮执行轨迹。大量实验表明，AgentLens实现了强大的安全检测性能，为前瞻性风险预判提供了初步证据，并显著减少了编码代理的有害行为，为将机制可解释性应用于动态LLM代理安全奠定了基础。代码可在：https://github.com/EddyLuo1232/AgentLens
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the safety risks associated with multi-turn interactions of coding agents based on large language models (LLMs), as existing safety mechanisms primarily rely on external guardrails that lack fine-grained control during execution. Previous methods have been limited to single-turn scenarios or jailbreak-style QA settings, failing to capture the evolving risk dynamics in multi-turn contexts. The proposed approach, AgentLens, introduces a white-box defense framework that detects harmful execution states from hidden representations and mitigates unsafe behavior by intervening in a 10-dimensional subspace, thus offering a more nuanced internal perspective on safety. This paper contributes by establishing the Mechanistic Agent Safety (MAS) benchmark, which includes annotated multi-turn execution trajectories across 194 tasks, and demonstrates that AgentLens significantly enhances safety detection performance and reduces harmful actions, supporting the goal of improving LLM agent safety in dynamic environments.

Enhancing LLM Safety Through a Theoretical Minimax Game Lens
Authors: Yihe Deng, Yu Yang, Junkai Zhang, Wei Wang, Bo Li
First: 2025-02-07T18:45:03+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-15T02:50:15+00:00
Comments: 24 pages, 9 figures, 5 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The rapid advancement of large language models (LLMs) necessitates effective mechanisms to ensure their responsible deployment by accurately distinguishing unsafe content from benign content. While substantial safety datasets are available in English, multilingual safety modeling remains underexplored due to limited open-source safety datasets in other languages. Even within English datasets, safe yet sensitive corner-case content is scarce, leading to shortcut learning by models and non-trivial false-positive rates. To mitigate these issues, we introduce a novel minimax reinforcement learning (RL) framework wherein a data generator and a classifier model co-evolve, facilitating the production of high-quality synthetic multilingual safety data. We theoretically formalize this interaction as a minimax game and rigorously demonstrate convergence to a Nash equilibrium. Empirical evaluations confirm that our synthetic data generation method significantly enhances the classifier model performance, enabling a substantially smaller model to surpass the state-of-the-art by nearly 10% on English benchmarks while achieving 4.5x faster inference speed. These results establish a scalable and efficient methodology for synthetic data generation, advancing the development of safer and more robust multilingual LLM deployments.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过理论最小最大博弈视角增强大型语言模型安全性
大型语言模型（LLMs）的快速发展需要有效机制，以确保其负责任的部署，准确区分不安全内容与良性内容。虽然英语中有大量安全数据集，但由于其他语言中开放源代码安全数据集有限，多语言安全建模仍未得到充分探索。即使在英语数据集中，安全但敏感的边缘案例内容也很稀缺，导致模型的捷径学习和非平凡的误报率。为了解决这些问题，我们引入了一种新颖的最小最大强化学习（RL）框架，其中数据生成器和分类模型共同进化，促进高质量合成多语言安全数据的生成。我们将这种交互理论化为最小最大博弈，并严格证明其收敛到纳什均衡。实证评估确认，我们的合成数据生成方法显著提升了分类模型的性能，使得一个显著更小的模型在英语基准测试中超越了最先进的技术近10%，同时实现了4.5倍的推理速度提升。这些结果建立了一种可扩展且高效的合成数据生成方法，推动了更安全、更强大的多语言LLM部署的发展。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the urgent need for effective safety mechanisms in large language models (LLMs) due to their rapid advancement and the challenges in distinguishing unsafe from benign content, particularly in multilingual contexts where safety datasets are limited. Previous methods have struggled with the scarcity of safe corner-case content, leading to shortcut learning and high false-positive rates. The proposed approach introduces a novel minimax reinforcement learning framework that allows a data generator and classifier to co-evolve, producing high-quality synthetic multilingual safety data and theoretically formalizing this interaction as a minimax game. This methodology contributes to the field by demonstrating a significant improvement in classifier performance, with a smaller model surpassing state-of-the-art benchmarks by nearly 10% in English and achieving 4.5 times faster inference speed, thus supporting the goal of safer multilingual LLM deployments.
本研究针对大型语言模型（LLMs）快速发展带来的安全机制需求，特别是在多语言环境中区分不安全与良性内容的挑战。以往的方法依赖于现有的安全数据集，这些数据集通常不足，导致模型出现捷径学习和高假阳性率等问题。提出的最小最大强化学习框架引入了数据生成器与分类器之间的共进化方法，将其互动形式化为最小最大博弈，以生成高质量的多语言安全合成数据。这一方法不仅提升了分类器模型的性能，使得更小的模型在英语基准测试中超越了现有技术近10%，并实现了4.5倍的推理速度提升，还为更安全的多语言LLM部署提供了可扩展的合成数据生成方法。
GAS-Leak-LLM: Genetic Algorithm-Based Suffix Optimization for Black-Box LLM Jailbreaking
Authors: Aman Anifer, Vignesh Kumar Kembu, Vishnu M, Antonino Nocera, Vinod P., Amal Murali PK, Akshay S Rajan
First: 2026-06-14T12:41:21+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-14T12:41:21+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) constitute pivotal components within the AI-dominated information technology ecosystem. To mitigate risks associated with harmful or policy-violating outputs, commercial systems employ advanced alignment strategies and multi-layered content moderation mechanisms. Despite these safeguards, recent research has demonstrated that LLMs remain vulnerable to adversarial manipulation, particularly through jailbreaking and prompt injection techniques. In this work, we propose GAS-Leak-LLM a novel jailbreaking attack based on a genetic algorithm that systematically evolves adversarial suffix to bypass safety constraints. Operating in a strict black-box setting, our method requires no access to model parameters or internals, thereby reflecting realistic threat scenarios in deployed systems. Through the iterative application of selection, mutation, and crossover heuristics, the framework systematically explores the discrete prompt space to identify high-fitness adversarial suffixes. Empirical findings reveal critical shortcomings in existing safety enforcement mechanisms and confirm the effectiveness and practical viability of the proposed attack.
中文标题/摘要
标题：GAS-Leak-LLM：基于遗传算法的黑箱LLM越狱后缀优化
大型语言模型（LLMs）是AI主导的信息技术生态系统中的关键组成部分。为了减轻与有害或违反政策输出相关的风险，商业系统采用先进的对齐策略和多层次内容审核机制。尽管有这些保护措施，最近的研究表明，LLMs仍然容易受到对抗性操控，特别是通过越狱和提示注入技术。在本研究中，我们提出了GAS-Leak-LLM，一种基于遗传算法的新型越狱攻击，系统地演化对抗性后缀以绕过安全约束。在严格的黑箱环境中，我们的方法不需要访问模型参数或内部结构，从而反映了已部署系统中的现实威胁场景。通过选择、突变和交叉启发式的迭代应用，该框架系统地探索离散提示空间，以识别高适应性的对抗性后缀。实证研究揭示了现有安全执行机制的关键缺陷，并确认了所提攻击的有效性和实际可行性。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to adversarial manipulation, particularly through jailbreaking and prompt injection techniques, despite existing alignment strategies and content moderation mechanisms. Previous methods have struggled to effectively mitigate these risks, leading to the development of GAS-Leak-LLM, a novel jailbreaking attack utilizing a genetic algorithm to evolve adversarial suffixes that bypass safety constraints without requiring access to model parameters. This approach is well-motivated as it reflects realistic threat scenarios in deployed systems and systematically explores the prompt space using selection, mutation, and crossover heuristics. The paper contributes by demonstrating the effectiveness and practical viability of this attack, revealing significant shortcomings in current safety enforcement mechanisms through empirical findings in a black-box setting.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）在面对对抗性操控时的脆弱性，尤其是越狱和提示注入技术，尽管已有安全措施。以往的方法依赖于各种对齐策略和内容审核机制，但这些措施在面对复杂攻击时显得不足。提出的方法GAS-Leak-LLM利用遗传算法在黑箱环境中进化对抗性后缀，从而在不需要访问模型内部的情况下绕过安全约束。该方法的动机合理，因为它反映了现实威胁场景，并通过选择、变异和交叉启发式系统地探索提示空间。实验结果显示当前安全执行机制的重大缺陷，并验证了GAS-Leak-LLM攻击的有效性，突显其在现实场景中的实用性。
CHILLGuard: Towards Fine-Grained Chinese LLM Safety Guardrail with Scalable Data Construction and Model-aware Preference Alignment
Authors: Wenbo Yu, Bohua Wang, Hao Fang, Kuofeng Gao, Jingru Zeng, Xiaochen Yang, Tianyi Zhang, Xiaoxiao Ma, Jiawei Kong, Hao Wu, Bin Chen, Shu-Tao Xia, Min Zhang
First: 2026-06-13T16:57:51+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-13T16:57:51+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Malicious content generated from large language models (LLMs) could pose severe safety risks and ethical concerns. While existing LLM safety guardrails excel in English or multilingual settings, they lack adaptation to Chinese-specific regulatory policies, cultural context and linguistic nuances, failing to support fine-grained risk classification for diverse deployment needs. In this paper, we introduce a 5-macro, 31-micro category fine-grained risk taxonomy for Chinese scenarios, and build CHILLGuard: a dedicated Chinese LLM content safety guardrail. To address the critical scarcity of high-quality annotated Chinese safety data, we propose a scalable multi-stage data construction pipeline: we expand multi-source corpus via retrieval-augmented generation, generate implicit harmful samples through prompt engineering rewriting, and refine high-quality data via multi-model voting-based label calibration. Based on this, we build CHILLGuardTrain, a large-scale training set with 405,007 samples, and CHILLGuardTest, a rigorously curated annotated test set with 51,745 samples. We then train CHILLGuard on CHILLGuardTrain under a generator-classifier collaborative framework via Model-aware Direct Preference Optimization. Extensive experiments under multiple settings demonstrate the state-of-the-art performance of CHILLGuard, e.g., a 15.92% improvement of F1 score over Qwen3Guard-8B-Strict on our benchmark. We will release our resources at https://github.com/cswbyu/CHILLGuard.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CHILLGuard：面向细粒度中文LLM安全防护的可扩展数据构建与模型感知偏好对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）生成的恶意内容可能带来严重的安全风险和伦理问题。现有的LLM安全防护在英语或多语言环境中表现出色，但缺乏对中国特定监管政策、文化背景和语言细微差别的适应，无法支持多样化部署需求的细粒度风险分类。本文提出了一种针对中文场景的5个宏观类别和31个微观类别的细粒度风险分类法，并构建了CHILLGuard：一个专门的中文LLM内容安全防护。为了解决高质量标注中文安全数据的严重短缺，我们提出了一种可扩展的多阶段数据构建流程：通过检索增强生成扩展多源语料，通过提示工程重写生成隐性有害样本，并通过多模型投票的标签校准精炼高质量数据。在此基础上，我们构建了CHILLGuardTrain，一个包含405,007个样本的大规模训练集，以及CHILLGuardTest，一个经过严格策划的标注测试集，包含51,745个样本。然后，我们在CHILLGuardTrain上通过模型感知直接偏好优化的生成器-分类器协作框架训练CHILLGuard。多种设置下的广泛实验表明，CHILLGuard的性能达到了最先进水平，例如，在我们的基准测试中，F1分数比Qwen3Guard-8B-Strict提高了15.92%。我们将把我们的资源发布在https://github.com/cswbyu/CHILLGuard。

The research addresses the safety risks and ethical concerns associated with malicious content generated by large language models (LLMs), particularly in the context of Chinese language applications where existing safety measures are inadequate. Previous methods have primarily focused on English or multilingual frameworks, lacking the necessary adaptation to Chinese regulatory policies and cultural nuances, which has resulted in ineffective risk classification. This paper proposes CHILLGuard, a dedicated Chinese LLM content safety guardrail, supported by a fine-grained risk taxonomy specifically designed for Chinese scenarios. The methodology includes a scalable multi-stage data construction pipeline that enhances the quality of annotated safety data through retrieval-augmented generation and prompt engineering, culminating in a large-scale training set of 405,007 samples. The experimental results demonstrate that CHILLGuard achieves state-of-the-art performance, including a 15.92% improvement in F1 score over existing models, thereby effectively addressing the identified gaps in LLM safety for Chinese contexts.
